Adding it would also red nothing today, since this file already carries `execFileSync` and the guard is FILE-SCOPED. **The cure for the harm is the sentence you are reading, at the site, which is s2400's own conclusion for `ps`/`kill`.** ⓘ **`git rev-parse` and `git cherry` are measured refused too and are prescribed in ZERO law surfaces — 0 occurrences across all six — so they are not owed a fallback at all; recorded only so the next reader does not re-measure them.** 📉 **AND THE WATCH ITSELF IS DISCHARGED, 245 FIRES AFTER s2182 (F-2427-2): `539 / 1198 = 45.0%` — the ABSOLUTE **FELL BY 2** (541 → 539) while the denominator grew **+76**, so the trigger (*"if it starts climbing, registration has stopped working"*) is **NOT TRIPPED** and the sequence 69.1% → 57.2% → 49.9% → 48.2% → **45.0%** continues to fall by dilution exactly as F-1538-1 predicted. **Instrument validated against TWO recorded trees before its answer was believed (F-2215-1): the crossing commit `176268426` reproduces `541/1084 = 49.9%` and s2182's own tree `b4c797684` reproduces `541/1122` — both to the digit.** 🔑 **THE SET DIFF, which F-2182-1 rightly demands whenever the number moves: 76 new masters, 0 removed, **3 legacy debts RETIRED** (`ap-orders-adapter-wiring.md`, `art-batch-roster-e9.md`, `art-e9-buildings-a.md` — **precisely the three UNKNOWNs F-2206-1 and F-2208-1 resolved by file probe**, so the debt F-2208-1 warned would red `dry-board-probe --strict` at rc=2 is now genuinely gone, corroborated by this fire's own probe reading `UNKNOWN: 0`), and exactly ONE newly-unregistered master — `e3-fairground-socket.md`. ⚖️ **THAT ONE IS NOT A VIOLATION AND MUST NOT BE COUNTED AS ONE: it is a bannered SUPERSEDED duplicate whose taskFile key an attended session REMOVED ON PURPOSE the same day it was written (`note_s_att_20260823`, readable via `drain-block-check`), retained under the RETENTION LAW because we supersede rather than erase. Real registration is 76/76.** 💡 **REUSABLE, and it is a measured LIMIT of this watch's own instrument: an exact-basename census scores a LAWFULLY leafless artifact as debt, so the ABSOLUTE can rise by one for a correct reason. A `+1` is a prompt to read the master, never a verdict that registration failed — ask `drain-block-check` before you alarm.** `--all` audits every blocked leaf on the board. ⏳ **AND RUN IT ONE STEP EARLIER IN TIME THAN THIS CLAUSE SAYS — BEFORE YOU WRITE A NEXT-FIRE INSTRUCTION THAT ASSERTS A TASK'S CLOSURE STATE, NOT MERELY BEFORE YOU DRAIN IT (F-1669-1, measured s1669).** This section's trigger is *the drain*, so it never fires for the fire that has nothing to drain — and that is exactly the fire that writes the handoff telling its successor what to drain. **s1665 asserted that `f1643-2` was a `gate-side` hold, citing as its evidence the DONE-MOVE FILENAME `stopped-s1655-gate-side-…`; s1667 and s1668 each copied the assertion forward.** The block had been lifted by **s1657 `9a32d94f2`**, whose diff deletes `status:"blocked"`, deletes `blockClass:"gate-side"`, deletes `blockedReason`, writes `status:"queued"` and **dispatches the master** — 1h52m after that filename froze. 🪦 **A done-move filename is minted once at done-move time and is NEVER renamed: it is a fossil of a claim the ledger can reverse, and here it did.** Measured across `tasks/done/`: **6 of 1270 filenames carry a closure-class token, 5 resolve to a leaf, and 2 of those 5 (40%) are contradicted by their live leaf** — this one (`queued`), and `…s1176-blocked-by-26-cell-master-divergence-…-eight-winds-wiring-hero.md`, whose leaf reads `merged` at `29bac3d9478e9f3a77385c75965b60447a90500c`. ⚠️ **The cost is not a stale paragraph — it is an armed Silent No-Op (Mistake #1):** the stopped run that filename names **left zero diff** by its own preserved record, so a successor obeying the instruction gates a run that changed nothing, and looks for a `blocked` leaf to flip that does not exist. 🚫 **No mechanism is proposed and the reason is the point: the obvious one — have this tool print the filename-vs-leaf disagreement — is cheap and purely mechanical and STILL would not have prevented this, because s1665 never ran the tool.** It had the filename in hand and read its words. **A tool only helps the fire that asks it** (the F-1582-1 gap: the misread happens while a fire reads with its eyes, a moment no battery observes). ➡️ **The cure is therefore the one command you already have, invoked at the moment of ASSERTION rather than the moment of action — and it generalises: any name the factory mints to describe a state (done-move prefixes, `save/` vs `archive/`, row markers) is a SNAPSHOT of a claim, while the ledger IS the claim. On disagreement the ledger wins and the name is what must be re-read.**
**§3.0b CUSTODY — NEVER PLACE CONTENT YOU HAVE NOT *DECIDED* TO MERGE INTO MAIN'S WORKING TREE. GATE UNDECIDED CONTENT IN A DETACHED WORKTREE (F-1295-1, s1295 — MADE STANDING s1342).** ⚠️ **This cure was declared "adopted" by exactly one fire and then written into NO law surface for 47 fires** — re-measured s1342 across `scripts/fire.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/skills/**`, `AGENTS.md` and every `^- **s9` bullet in `STATUS.md` (**140 total = 18 genuine lettered law bullets** — `s9i`/`s9ab`/`s9al`/`s9ar`/… — **plus 119 numeric handoff archives and 3 bare `- **s9 ` VM-era env bullets (obsolete on native, per the blanket VM-era clause)**; the loose phrase "140 law bullets" would over-state the law corpus 7.8×, so it is written out here — ✅ **RE-MEASURED s1538, 196 fires later: EXACTLY 140 / 18 / 119 / 3, every figure unchanged. DO NOT RE-DERIVE THIS ONE AGAIN.** It is stable *by construction*, which is the part worth knowing: the `^- **s9` pattern matches only the closed historical range s900–s999, so it cannot grow no matter how many fires run — today's archives are minted as `- **s15NN`. A figure that looks like a growing census is in fact a frozen one, and this line now says so): the only "detached worktree" in the corpus is `CLAUDE.md:50` (Mistake #12), which is about **contention**, a different question F-1295-1 itself explicitly distinguishes. §3's clean-main rule asks whether *someone else's* dirt contaminates your gate; **this asks the inverse — whether YOUR gate leaks.** 🎯 **THE INCIDENT:** s1294 checked four lane paths into main's tree to evaluate a slice, wrote **VERDICT: HOLD — NOT MERGED**, reverted with `git checkout HEAD -- <paths>`, confirmed `git status` clean, and was **wrong** — a concurrent attended session's broad `git add` had already committed all four at **`b37c1fc6`**, under a commit message about a rehearsal that named none of them, **thirteen minutes before the handoff denying it.** 🔑 **WHY THE VERIFICATION COULD NOT WORK, WHICH IS THE REUSABLE HALF: every probe that fire had returns the SAME answer whether the revert worked or the content was committed out from under it.** `git status` cannot detect a sweep, because **a sweep's signature is the ABSENCE of dirt**; and `git checkout HEAD -- <paths>` restores *from* a HEAD that has itself moved, so the "revert" **re-installed** the very content it was undoing. The only discriminating probe is a **blob-hash comparison against a PRE-sweep ref** — which is why F-1295-1 is recorded in hashes. ⚖️ **A HOLD verdict has NO ENFORCEMENT SURFACE:** it lives in a review file and a goal leaf, and **nothing in git defends it**. The same window ships an **owner-BLOCKED** slice just as readily — the version that costs a design fork, and the one §3.0 exists to prevent. **What must NOT be concluded is "the sweep turned out fine, so the sweep is fine":** that slice was exonerated by a control run *afterwards*, and had it gone the other way the defect would have shipped past a verdict that said no, with the fire that wrote the verdict still believing it held. Cost of the cure: one `git worktree add` plus a `node_modules` symlink. ⓘ **SCOPE, STATED HONESTLY SO THE NEXT READER DOES NOT OVER-CORRECT ME: F-1295-1 measured the WORKING-TREE case only.** Gating by `git merge --no-ff` and planning to revert on a red (§3, "revert the merge") puts undecided content on **HEAD** rather than in the working tree — structurally the same bet against concurrency, but **UNMEASURED as of s1342**; treat it as a caution, not a finding, and do not cite it as proven. ✅ **NO LONGER UNMEASURED — THE HEAD-SIDE CASE WAS MEASURED s1589 AND THE CAUTION IS NOW A FINDING (F-1589-5).** s1589 left drain 2's merge staged in main's index while writing its review; **ninety seconds later** a concurrent Cowork agent committed **`9371c1c8d`** — parents `e15858b13` (the fire's own stamp commit) and `153d30ba6` (the lane tip) — sweeping the entire staged merge into a commit headlined *"the operator falls at twin-banks w16 (697 kills) — notebook gen 2"*, which names none of it. ⓘ **Nothing was lost and nothing was corrupt** (`main..lane/b` empty, artifacts byte-identical, the fire's other drain untouched) — **the casualty was attribution, and the EXPOSURE is far worse than the outcome: had that gate gone RED, the plan was `merge --abort`, and the sweep would have SHIPPED the rejected content anyway.** That is the same failure as F-1295-1 with the staging area swapped for the index, and it retires the "structurally the same bet" hedge above: it is not merely structurally the same, it has now happened. ➡️ **THEREFORE, BINDING: never leave a merge staged on main.** Gate undecided content in the detached worktree exactly as this section already mandates, then **merge and commit as ONE act** — `git merge --no-ff <branch> -m "…"`, never `--no-commit` followed by review-writing. The review file, ledger rows and goal leaf land in a FOLLOWING commit; that is already the house sequence (§3.0's two-commit rule exists because a commit cannot contain its own hash), so this costs nothing and closes the window. ⚠️ **Do not read "the sweep turned out fine" as evidence the window is safe** — that is the exact reasoning this section already forbids one paragraph above, and s1589 is the second fire to be saved by luck rather than by design.
**§3.1 EVERY FIRE-SIDE PLAYWRIGHT COMMAND PASSES `--workers=1`. NOT AN OPTIMISATION — A CORRECTNESS REQUIREMENT OF THE INSTRUMENT (F-1270-1, measured s1270).** Until `d1a0846d` `playwright.config.ts` set no `workers` key, so a fire's default was **6 obtained workers**, and at 6 the fire shell's per-job CPU ceiling (F-1269-1: the ceiling is the fire's process context, measured 7.53× lane vs 3.47× fire at eight children) starves each chromium until timing-sensitive assertions go red. **Interleaved, same shell, same hour, s1270: `--workers=1` → 3/3 runs rc=0, 18/18 instances passed, 0 drift reds · default → 3/3 runs rc=1, 17 drift reds / 18.** ⭐ **And it costs nothing, which kills the only objection: w=1 mean wall 55.94 s vs default 60.74 s — the serial arm is 7.9% FASTER in the fire shell**, because the parallel arm is fighting the ceiling. ⚠️ **This is a factory-gate correctness issue, not a game bug: every fire-side gate ever run at default workers was a known-unreliable instrument, and its reds were the shell's, not the slice's.** `.claude/skills/drain/SKILL.md:33` has carried the flag since s114 — incidentally, with no stated reason, which is exactly why six fires never knew it was load-bearing; this clause states the reason so it cannot decay again. ✅ **THE LAW NOW HAS A MECHANISM, SO THE FLAG IS A BELT-AND-BRACES, NOT THE ONLY DEFENCE (F-1270-3, landed s1270 `d1a0846d`): `playwright.config.ts:50` carries `workers: isFireShell ? 1 : undefined`, keyed on `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` being PRESENT** (**was `:30`; re-based s1301 when the F-1296-3 `testIgnore` comment block was inserted above it — cite the CODE, the coordinate drifts) — launchd sets it to `~/.claude-fires` and `scripts/fire-runner.sh:138 (re-based 133→138, +5, on 2026-09-14 by the maps-campaign-land-era6 drain: the FIRE_ENGINE default switched back to claude with a five-line owner-verbatim comment above it; substance re-verified by READING)` (**was `:129` — re-based s2500 by RE-GREPPING the cited content (`129 → 130`, **+1**), the rot caused by the ATTENDED commit `304ff2396` (2026-09-04T12:45:12, the codex engine switch) landing MID-FIRE and inserting the WALL-on-primary echo directly above it. The cited line is INTACT and still supports the claim — re-read s2500: `:130` is the `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR="$HOME/.claude-alt"` alt-fire invocation, so `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is still SET for fires and the `workers: isFireShell` key still holds. ⚠️ **Two sibling pointers rotted by the same +1 in the same commit (`CLAUDE.md` → `:136→:137`, `.claude/skills/drain/SKILL.md` → `:129→:130`) — re-grep EACH, never carry one delta across three surfaces; they agreed here only by luck of a single-line insertion.** `:126`, `:121` same-day (+5,+3), `:102`, `:86`, `:81` before that; rotted attended 2026-08-12 by exactly 19 lines when the owner-ruled fire-engine switch landed above it, ALT line re-verified intact; before that rotted s1536 by exactly 16 lines when the F-1536-1 dry-board-guard cure replaced the hardcoded lane list at `:63`, and before that s1456 by 5 lines when the owner's `02bea28c` auth fix inserted the `.env.local` token block above it — cite the CODE, the coordinate drifts**) to `~/.claude-alt`, while lanes and attended sessions never set it, so **lanes keep full parallelism** (the lane shell runs 6 workers ~3.5× FASTER — F-1267-1 — and must not pay for a fire-only defect). Still pass `--workers=1` explicitly: it is free, it survives anyone editing the config, and it keeps the intent legible at the call site. 🚫 **What remains forbidden is pinning `workers` UNCONDITIONALLY** — a flat `workers: 1` taxes every lane run, and it passes a fire-only check while doing so, which is why the guard below asserts BOTH directions. ⚠️ **DO NOT "restore" this law by deleting the `workers: isFireShell` line** — an earlier revision of this very clause read *"`playwright.config.ts` sets no `workers` key"* and *"Do NOT fix this by setting `workers` in `playwright.config.ts`"*, both written **before** the mechanism landed in the same fire; a session obeying that sentence literally would silently re-open F-1270-1. 🔒 **Guarded since s1273 (F-1273-1): `scripts/fire-shell-serialisation.test.mjs`** in `test:node-guards` resolves the config in both env states and reds if the fire shell stops serialising **or** if lanes start being pinned. ⚖️ **A red seen at default workers is not evidence until it reproduces at `--workers=1`.**
For a drain: `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npm run build` + the slice's own spec + affected adjacent suites + a boot probe (zero console/page errors, desktop + 390px). Full native runs — no splits needed. 🧮 **AND IF THE SLICE'S DIFF TOUCHES `src/sim/`, `src/systems/` OR `src/entities/`, THE BATTERY ALSO INCLUDES `npm run test:node-guards` (F-1460-1, second half of the cure; landed s1462 with the re-pin).** A slice-local spec is structurally incapable of seeing a **cross-cutting sim pin**, because the pin does not live in the slice's own suite — so a drain gated correctly and completely on the slice's specs can still move a number that another guard asserts, and nothing in the prescribed battery will say so. ⚠️ **That is not hypothetical, it is how F-1460-1 was born:** `4ab48743` (f1452-1 fort-solidity — gates the stuck-watchdog on `route.blocker` in `src/entities/Enemy.ts`, +139/-31 in `src/systems/BuildSystem.ts`) changed routing, therefore engagement, therefore **8 fewer kills over 20 waves**; it was deliberate and spec-green and reached main via drain `07213c73`, whose evidence reads *"4/4 own specs both projects"*. The Baron pin in `scripts/gr-sim.test.mjs` went stale that instant, `test:node-guards` went red **on every fire from 2026-08-04 to s1462**, and — the expensive part — **the red was wearing an excused class label (`cross-engine`), so for five fires nobody investigated it.** A red nobody investigates is worse than no test. 💡 **The general shape: a change is cross-cutting when it moves BEHAVIOUR the sim replays, not when it edits many files** — routing, blockers, targeting, spawn order and damage resolution all qualify while touching two files. Judge by the directory, not by the diff size; that is why this rule is keyed on paths a human cannot forget rather than on a judgement call. ⓘ **Cost, measured s1462 and stated honestly rather than waved at:** `scripts/gr-sim.test.mjs` is the battery's dominant term at **55.6 s** (its Baron test alone 8.5 s); the full battery is **284 tests / 281 pass / 3 skipped** and runs in one command. That is cheap against two days of a red board. 📈 **RE-MEASURED s1537 (F-1537-1) — every number in the previous sentence has drifted, and the DIRECTION is what matters: the battery is now `363 tests / 360 pass / 0 fail / 3 skipped` in `181.3 s`, and `gr-sim` alone is `12 tests / 79.9 s` (44% of the whole), its two Baron tests `15.0 s` + `14.2 s` where the old text says one at 8.5 s.** ⚠️ **Do not read the drift as rot in the pointer sense — the battery GREW on purpose (+79 tests since s1462) and the s1462 figures were honest when written; what is now false is only the CHEAPNESS ARGUMENT'S ARITHMETIC, so it is restated rather than deleted.** ⓘ Practical consequence for the fire that has to schedule it: **3 minutes serial, not one**, and a fire that overlaps it with a second battery will contaminate both (s1536 hung ~19 min doing exactly that on shared `claimed-spec-harness-guard`/`fixture-teardown` fixtures). **Run it ALONE.** ⚠️ **AND THE REASON IS NOT THE HANG — A SELF-CONTAMINATED BATTERY MANUFACTURES FINDINGS, AND ITS RED IS NOT EVIDENCE IN EITHER DIRECTION (F-2462-1, measured s2462).** The sentence above justifies running alone with a HANG (s1536, ~19 min on shared fixtures), which is a COST and reads as a scheduling nicety a hurried fire may trade away. **The load-bearing reason is that a fire's own parallelism is INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A REAL TIMING REGRESSION AT THE ASSERTION.** 📊 **MEASURED s2462 on ONE tree, the only variable being what else was running: `test:power-budget` returned **p95 0.966 ms against a 0.500 ms cap — FAIL** while a playwright battery ran beside it, and **0.334 ms — PASS** (then 0.384 ms) alone. A **2.9× swing on a timing assertion**, against a slice that does not touch `src/systems/PowerGraph.ts` at all.** ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: NOTHING SHIPPED WRONG and this is not a false green — the contradiction was visible because the same tree was measured twice, and the cost was one wasted battery.** 🎯 **What earns it a clause is the DIRECTION: a fire that reported that run would have filed a FABRICATED REGRESSION — a real cap, a real p95, a real red, and no defect — against another agent's slice.** 🔁 **AND IT CUTS BOTH WAYS ACROSS A HANDOFF, measured in the same fire (F-2462-3): the RUNNER's own headline for this battery read *"558 pass, 5 fail"* where the drain's re-run ALONE read **564 pass / 2 fail / 5 skipped** — three of its five reds were its shell's contention. **Contention inflates RED COUNTS on both sides of a handoff**, so the drain's own re-run is a free control on the runner's headline (F-2166-2) and a runner's reds are a claim (Mistake #4) exactly as its greens are.** 🚫 **No guard is proposed and the restraint is measured: "was anything else running?" is not observable to a battery — the assertion sees only a number — so the cure is this sentence, at the site, read at the moment it applies.** It is still cheap against two days of a red board. 📈 **RE-MEASURED s2099 (F-2099-1) — THE SAME DRIFT AGAIN, NOW 2.2× THE s1537 FIGURE, AND THE SCHEDULING SENTENCE JUST ABOVE IS THE PART THAT WENT FALSE: the battery is `472 tests / 467 pass / 0 fail / 0 cancelled / 5 skipped` in `404.7 s` — SEVEN minutes serial, not three.** Fire shell, node **v26.4.0**, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set, file concurrency 1, run ALONE on a dry board at load avg 3.75. `gr-sim.test.mjs` is **still** the dominant term and has grown with the battery: **17 tests / 227.3 s / 56.2% of wall** (was 12 / 79.9 s / 44%); the runner-up is a SINGLE test — fixture-teardown's *"all 34 scripts/\*.test.mjs fixture owners remove their temp directories"* at **69.6 s** — and **14 tests over 5 s account for 313.5 s, 77% of wall**, so the cost is concentrated, not diffuse. ⓘ **The s1462 and s1537 figures above are RESTATED, NOT DELETED, because the SEQUENCE is the finding:** 284 → 363 → 424 (s1613) → 472 tests, and 55.6 → 181.3 → 280.9 → 404.7 s. **It only ever drifts upward, so a fire reading a stale number under-budgets the duty by exactly the amount the factory has grown** — which is why this correction is now the third of its kind and should be expected to be re-taken again, not treated as final. 📈 **RE-MEASURED s2166 (F-2166-2) — AND IT WAS RE-TAKEN, EXACTLY AS PREDICTED, ONE FIRE-DAY LATER: the battery is `503` tests / `498 pass / 0 fail / 5 skipped` in **`529.8 s`** — NINE minutes serial, not seven.** Fire shell, node **v26.4.0**, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set, run ALONE on the merged tree of the `c1-assay-fairness` drain. **The sequence is now 284 → 363 → 424 → 472 → `503` tests and 55.6 → 181.3 → 280.9 → 404.7 → `529.8` s** — RESTATED, NOT DELETED, because the sequence IS the finding and the monotone direction is the whole content. ⓘ **This measurement also carries a second, load-bearing result: rc=0.** The runner that produced the slice reported `test:node-guards` **non-green** and blamed its own shell (node **23.11.1** against `.nvmrc` **26.4.0**, one 90 s `gr-sim` file timeout); re-run ALONE in the fire shell the SAME tree is **rc=0, 0 fail**, and `gr-sim` alone is **20 tests / 18 pass / 0 fail / 2 skipped / 265.8 s**. That is the F-2076-1 mechanism confirmed a third time from the other direction — **v23 bounds at FILE granularity and never consults per-test budgets** — so a lane-side red on this battery is a question about WHICH INTERPRETER RAN IT before it is a question about the slice. **The drain's own re-run is the free control on the runner's headline; take it before you inherit a red.** ⚖️ **AND DO NOT INHERIT "the battery reds in the fire shell" FROM F-2076-1 — THAT RED IS LOAD-ATTRIBUTABLE, MEASURED, NOT STRUCTURAL.** All three tests it named as budget-endangered PASSED here with 2.0–2.4× more headroom than it recorded one day earlier: `runtime rush` (90 s budget) **90,003 ms CANCELLED at s2076 → 46,297 ms PASS (51.4%)**, `overtime`-on-both-engines (240 s) **219,418 ms (91.4%) → 91,282 ms (38.0%)**, `Twin Banks` (45 s) **34,896 ms (77.5%) → 16,275 ms (36.2%)**. F-2076-1's MECHANISM stands unrefuted and is the durable half (v26 consults per-test budgets, v23 bounds at FILE granularity and never consults them); what is now measured is that the MARGIN moves with machine load, so a red here is a question about the arrangement before it is a question about the sim — **`sample` the pid and check the load before theorising, and never re-pin to make it go away.** ⚖️ **A failure here is a FINDING, never a re-pin reflex** — see the standing prohibition below and the comment at the pin site: re-pin only with a **named cause**, never to make a red go away (F-1441-3). **Merge-throughput tools (2026-07-06): (a) DISJOINT-PAIR BATCH — two lane drains may share ONE gate battery when their `git diff --name-only` sets vs their bases are disjoint (additive-only Balance.ts / vite-env.d.ts hunks excepted): merge both onto clean main, run one combined battery (both slices' specs + the union of adjacent suites), commit each path-scoped. On ANY failure revert both and fall back to strict serial. Never batch more than two; never batch when one touches files the other's spec exercises. (b) ⛔ **WITHDRAWN s1270 — this clause prescribed the exact thing §3.1 forbids.** It read: "the desktop and mobile playwright projects may run concurrently on two scratch ports when wall-time matters." Both of its premises are now measured false in the fire shell: concurrency is what MANUFACTURES the reds (17 drift reds / 18 at default workers vs 0 / 18 at `--workers=1`), and wall-time does not favour it anyway (**60.74 s concurrent vs 55.94 s serial**). Run the projects SERIALLY under `--workers=1`. The scratch-port family (5199/5231/5234) stays — that is Mistake #12 attribution hygiene and is unrelated. The gate CONTENT is never thinned — these optimize repetition, not rigor.** For lane drains: diff the worktree against ITS base commit (find it via the lane branch), classify LANE-TOUCHED vs MAIN-MOVED-ONLY per file, never blind-copy — 3-way graft when both moved. Merge onto CLEAN main only. Write `reviews/<slice>.md` (verdict, evidence, findings F-<id>, env exceptions with proof) + screenshots to `reviews/shots-<slice>/`. Findings that block: write a corrective task to tasks/queue/<slot>/ instead of merging. Art drains: QA raws vs the request's measured criteria, `node scripts/extract-alpha.mjs --key <hex> --grid CxR`, seam-law height checks, wire contracts, in-game screenshot review, LEDGER update.
Commit style: slice-scoped message with gates summary (see git log for the house voice). Path-scoped adds ONLY.

## 4. Handoff (mandatory when you took the lock)
Rewrite STATUS.md line-1: `Last updated: <ISO> s<N> handoff, lock CLEARED — <what landed + evidence pointers + what's next (A/B/C/... priorities for the next fire)>`. Move the previous line-1 to a `- **s<N-1> handoff (line-1 archive):** ...` bullet below the law bullets. ⚠️ **THE PRIOR HANDOFF MUST END UP ARCHIVED — CHECK, DO NOT ASSUME YOUR TOOLING DOES IT.** Dropping the previous line-1 when you take the LOCK and re-archiving it in the handoff commit is the **normal** pattern and `scripts/status-archive-audit.mjs` scores it as such (it reported *"7 dropped at lock time but restored by the fire's own handoff commit — normal, not a defect"* on the very run that checked this fire) — so the duty is about the END STATE, not the order. ⏱️ **AMENDED s2403 (F-2403-1) — THAT LAST CLAUSE IS TRUE ONLY FOR A FIRE THAT SURVIVES TO WRITE ITS HANDOFF, AND THE ONE CASE WHERE IT IS FALSE IS THE CASE §1.1 ALREADY PRICES.** When a fire dies between its lock commit and its handoff, there IS no handoff commit to restore anything — so the ORDER is precisely what decides whether its predecessor's line-1 survives in the file, and this sentence tells a fire the opposite. 🔗 **THE SHARPER HALF, WHICH §1.1 DOES NOT COVER: ITS RECOVERY DUTY IS WRITTEN AS A SINGLE STEP** — *"a fire killed between its lock commit and its handoff leaves the PREVIOUS fire's line-1 dropped and unarchived, so the takeover owes that recovery too"* — **so a CONSECUTIVE CASCADE strands the fire TWO BACK, and nobody owns it.** The taking fire recovers its immediate predecessor, correctly, and stops. 📊 **MEASURED s2403 over the 12 most recent dead fires: 11 were recovered correctly and exactly ONE is a gap — and it is exactly the cascade case.** s2395 died mid-drain (`FIRE END rc=0` 14:30:05); s2396 took over at 14:36, dropping s2395's line-1 per the §4-**normal** pattern this clause blesses, and itself died at 14:42:54; s2397 took over at 14:49, archived s2396's lock line — its bullet still reads *"dead fire, no handoff — archived by s2397"* — and did not walk back one further. **s2395's line-1 was absent from STATUS.md at HEAD for ~5 h, recovered VERBATIM from `64b9817a1` by s2403.** ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: NOTHING WAS LOST, and this is NOT a false green.** `STATUS.md` is TRACKED, so git holds every version and the RETENTION LAW explicitly permits compaction of tracked files; the era-5 drain s2395 died running was finished by s2397; and **`status-archive-audit`'s `CLEAN` is CORRECT, not blind** — it scopes to HANDOFF line-1s, and s2395 never wrote a handoff, so it was right to say nothing. 🎯 **What earns it a clause is the DIRECTION and the SURFACE: the archive chain is what a fire reads at TRIAGE, and it showed an unbroken `s2397 → s2396 → s2394` with no marker that a third fire had died mid-drain between them.** A fire reconstructing that afternoon from the file alone would not know s2395 existed. ➡️ **THEREFORE, BINDING, ON ANY DEAD-LOCK TAKEOVER: walk the chain BACKWARD until you reach a line-1 that IS archived — one step is not enough when your predecessor died too.** It is one command per session: `grep -c "s<N> " STATUS.md`. 🛠️ **AND THE DISCIPLINE THAT PREVENTS THE WHOLE CLASS COSTS ONE SPLICE — ARCHIVE YOUR PREDECESSOR IN YOUR *LOCK* COMMIT, NOT AT HANDOFF.** The end state §4 requires is identical; only the window differs, and closing it removes both the stranding above and the mid-fire `LOST: 1` that `status-archive-audit` otherwise reports for a fire's entire working life. **Verified s2403: locking this way, the audit reads `CLEAN: 0 permanently absent` while the lock is still held.** 📊 **BUT IT LIVED NOWHERE A FIRE READS: censused over the six `LAW_SURFACES` (imported from `scripts/law-surfaces.mjs`, not transcribed) the cure scored **ZERO** hits — the single hit in `fire.md` is F-2204-1 describing the HAZARD — while adoption ran at **36 of the 60 most recent lock commits (60.0%) and OSCILLATED fire to fire** (s2401 dropped · s2400 archived · s2399 dropped · s2398 dropped · s2397 archived). That oscillation is the signature of a discipline carried in handoff prose: each fire re-derives it, or does not, depending on what its predecessor happened to mention. 💡 **This is the factory's most-repeated finding for the SIXTH time (F-2153-1 · F-2204-1 · F-2350-1 · F-2360-1 · F-2365-1) — a known cure with no reader in the surface that governs the moment it applies — and F-2204-1 diagnosed this exact cure as parked outside the law 199 fires ago.** 🚫 **NO GUARD, and the restraint is measured rather than lazy: the drop-until-handoff pattern is LAWFUL and this clause keeps blessing it, so a red on "did not archive in the lock commit" would fire during ordinary correct operation and be excused into uselessness inside a week (F-1460-1, the `cross-engine` fate). ⓘ The hazard's own reachability is honest too — 16 self-declared dead-lock takeovers in 875 lock commits (1.8%) — but it CLUSTERS: five of them fall in the last ~40 fires, and two were consecutive on 2026-08-31.** The trap is a handoff step that archives **the wrong line**: s1472's replace-then-archive helper would have archived its own LOCK line and left s1471's entire handoff in no bullet at all (caught and recovered verbatim from `f3f2f01b5`). **Before committing the handoff, `grep -c "s<N-1> handoff (line-1 archive)" STATUS.md` and expect 1.** 🗂️ **AND THE HANDOFF MUST END IN AN EXPLICIT, GREPPABLE DESK SEGMENT — this is now MECHANISED and it will REFUSE (exit 2), not warn (F-1471-3 cured s1472).** Write the desk at the END of line-1 as `🔺 **OWNER'S DESK — <n> awaiting a word.** 🔺 **F-xxxx-x** …`; any of `OWNER DESK` / `OWNER'S DESK` / `OWNERS DESK` (straight or curly apostrophe) is matched, and the LAST occurrence on line-1 wins, so an upstream prose mention is harmless. **If the desk is genuinely EMPTY, still write the header** — `test:desk-declaration` cannot tell "no items" from "I could not read the desk", and it now refuses rather than green over the difference. ⓘ **WHY THIS BECAME A HARD GATE, because it is a real change in fire duty and should not read as pedantry:** the guard used to search the WHOLE file for one spelling and take the FIRST hit, so a headerless or possessively-spelled line-1 silently resolved to an ARCHIVED desk — and archived desks are by construction already declared, so it reported a clean board every time it read the wrong one. It fails OPEN, which is how it passed for 137 fires while 4 desk items sat undeclared, three of them buried in rows marked ✅ SHIPPED. 📉 **The deeper measurement (F-1472-1): the desk header is a LAPSED convention — of 1249 line-1s in STATUS.md only 617 carry a desk word, and among the last ~55 fires roughly 5 did.** The rest route owner items as bare `🔺 **F-xxxx-x OPEN**` runs inside a `NEXT: (A)/(B)/(C)` paragraph, where nothing can check them. The bug and the lapsed convention hid each other, which is precisely why neither was noticed. ✅ Mid-fire the guard SKIPs on an `ACTIVE` lock line (so drain batteries are unaffected); the gate bites in `test:ledger-guards`, your last act. 🔎 **AND BEFORE YOU DECIDE WHAT TO CARRY, ASK THE INSTRUMENT — `node scripts/desk-state-audit.mjs` (shipped s1566 `6001f3a5`, from F-1561-5 with its cure re-scoped).** It classifies every carried desk item `CLOSED` / `OPEN` / `BOTH` / `OPEN-DESK-ONLY` / `UNRECORDED`, printing the BACKLOG line numbers that justify each verdict, and resolves backticked goal slugs from `tasks/goals.json` with their `blockClass`. Classification is **subject-first** — a row states the state only of the FIRST F-ID in its 90-char subject zone, so an id merely CITED inside another finding's subject carries no state; that rule was checked against all three live conflicts before it shipped. It is **advisory, exit 0 always**, by the `drain-block-check` UNKNOWN precedent; `--strict` exits 1 only when a carried item is `CLOSED` and therefore should have left the desk. ⚠️ **It cannot read your own line-1 while you hold the lock** — line 1 carries `ACTIVE` for a fire's entire working life and the tool prints `SKIP` (F-1566-1, non-blocking: a lock line genuinely has no desk). 🕳️ **AND FOR HUNDREDS OF FIRES IT DID NOT — THE SENTENCE YOU JUST READ WAS FALSE UNTIL s2227, AND THE WORD THAT MADE IT FALSE WAS `begins` (F-2227-1, measured and cured s2227).** This clause used to read *"line 1 **begins** `ACTIVE`"*, and so did the code: the lock test now at `desk-state-audit.mjs:72` — **was `:68` s2227–s2262 (re-based s2263, +4, when the F-2263-1 `FINDING_ALL` derivation landed above it — the SECOND rot of this coordinate and the SECOND RUNNING caused by a fire editing the very script the law cites, predicted by this clause and cured in the same landing commit), and `:42` before that; re-based by RE-GREPPING the content, never by adding a remembered delta; cite the CODE, the coordinate drifts** — read `line1.startsWith('ACTIVE')`. **That convention is RETIRED.** Today's lock line reads `Last updated: <stamp> ACTIVE (sNNNN fire) — <intent>`, which does not START with the word — so the `kind:'lock'` branch was UNREACHABLE and every lock line fell through to the `kind:'none'` arm: **`REFUSING — no desk header on line-1` at rc=2**, a message that accuses the wrong subject, since the line has no desk header BECAUSE it is a lock. 📊 **MEASURED over all 4,639 STATUS.md commits: the predicate its three siblings share (`/\bACTIVE\b/ && !/lock CLEARED/`) identifies 2,119 lock lines; `startsWith` identifies 1,696 — 423 disagreements, and the disagreement is CURRENT.** ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND NOT INFLATED: NOT a false green, NOT a Mistake #8 clearance.** The direction was CONSERVATIVE — a loud refusal — and this advisory is chained bare in NO battery (only its `.test.mjs` is a leg), so nothing ever redded and no verdict was wrong. What rotted was the DOCUMENTED behaviour, in this clause and in `gate-caller-baseline.json`'s F-1566-2 entry, which BOTH promised a benign `SKIP`. ⓘ **F-1566-2's CONCLUSION — never root this advisory in a battery — SURVIVES its refuted reason and is STRENGTHENED by it: an rc=2 refusal would have redded any battery that chained it.** 🔑 **WHY IT ROTTED IS THE REUSABLE HALF, AND IT NAMES A BLIND SPOT IN THIS STREAK'S OWN METHOD: a predicate can drift for hundreds of fires precisely BECAUSE its failure is loud and safe, so nobody is ever forced to look at it.** Every discriminator built since s2204 hunts a corpus that goes silently EMPTY; this one went loudly WRONG, and no census keyed on swallows, handlers, guards or trees can see that shape. 🛠️ **CURE: import the sibling predicate rather than write a fifth copy** — `desk-carryforward-guard.mjs` already imports `subjectLedClosure` FROM `desk-state-audit.mjs`, so the cycle predates the change, and F-1261-1's rule (*"there is one implementation of that word in this repo"*) is the same reason that import exists. **Four independent copies of one predicate is HOW this drifted**, so the guard asserts all four agree rather than testing the cured file alone. ✅ **BEHAVIOUR-NEUTRAL BEFORE LANDING (F-1274-2): the PRESCRIBED `--status <inherited handoff>` path is 3/3 BYTE-IDENTICAL across default/`--strict`/`--json`; only the two lock-line arms move, and they move TO the `SKIP` this clause promises.** 🔒 **`scripts/desk-lock-predicate-guard.test.mjs` — 8 arms, rooted in `test:ledger-guards` (battery GREEN, 368 assertions / 0 fail, up from 360).** 🔬 **TEETH: pre-cure verbatim reds 3 of 8 with all four reverse controls green; INVERTED reds 5; NEVER-a-lock reds 4; and the OVER-GENERAL cure — dropping the `lock CLEARED` clause, which would make every desk gate SKIP on every handoff and retire the whole desk-gating layer — reds ARM 4 ALONE.** ⚠️ **One prediction corrected (I expected that variant to red four arms), and per s2226's duty the sweep doubled as a reachability audit: ALL 8 ARMS red in at least one manufactured variant, so none is decoration.** 💡 **AND THE TRAP THAT NEARLY HID THIS, WHICH BIT TWICE IN ONE FIRE AND IS THE THING TO CARRY FORWARD: F-2215-1's validity assertion — *"did the control arm PRODUCE anything?"* — ASKS WHETHER THE TOOL RAN, NOT WHETHER THE VERDICT PATH RAN.** s2227's root-vs-worktree sweep of the three desk guards read `INVARIANT` on all three at 167 B per arm and was **VACUOUS**: the fire held the lock, so all three printed their legitimate `SKIP` and it had compared three pairs of SKIPS. **A SKIP is not silence — it DEFEATS the s2224 agreeing-silences rule by SATISFYING it.** The same trap then bit the guard itself: arm 8 asserts an rc=2 refusal, and this tool resolves `--backlog`/`--goals` **CWD-relatively** (`desk-state-audit.mjs:235` — **was `:180` s2227–s2262, re-based s2263 by +55 when the F-2263-1 `nonSubjectMentions` block landed above it; cite the CODE, the coordinate drifts**), so off the repo root it refused with ENOENT at **the same exit code** and the arm passed for the wrong reason until its MESSAGE assertion caught it. ➡️ **Assert that your arm REACHED THE BRANCH you are measuring, and assert the OBSERVABLE rather than the exit code alone.** 📋 **A SIBLING MEASUREMENT BANKED, NOT CURED (F-2227-2): `test:ledger-guards` exits 0 IDENTICALLY whether its three desk gates evaluated or SKIPPED** — measured mid-fire on the live board, `rc=0` with all three printing the lock SKIP, i.e. three verdict-bearing legs checking nothing while the aggregate code stays silent. **Deliberately NOT cured: the SKIP is correct, and making it refuse would red every lawful mid-fire battery (F-1460-1).** The ordering law already cures it and this was VERIFIED rather than assumed — replaying the **20 most recent REAL handoff commits** through the two file-based desk guards gives **20/20 evaluated, 0 SKIPs, 0 flagged**. ➡️ **So the owed act is a READING HABIT: when the battery is green, check the three desk legs say `PASS`, not `SKIP`. A `SKIP` means you ran it before your handoff commit and the desk is still ungated — re-run it after.** **So point it at what you INHERITED:** extract the previous fire's archived line-1 (the `- **s<N-1> handoff (line-1 archive):** …` bullet, strip that prefix) into a temp file and pass `--status <that file>`. That is the question a handoff actually asks — *what did I inherit, and is any of it already answered?* 📊 **First real run, s1566, over s1565's 22 carried items against main's `BACKLOG.md` and `goals.json`: `CLOSED=0 · OPEN=5 · BOTH=0 · OPEN-DESK-ONLY=17 · UNRECORDED=0`** — nothing was carried that should have been dropped, and no carried item is a ghost. ✅ **THIS RETIRES A STANDING DISCLAIMER: s1561, s1563 and s1564 each carried 22 items forward with an explicit "I did NOT re-verify each item's closure state" note, because no instrument could answer it.** A fire that writes that sentence now is declining a tool it has. ⓘ **A `CLOSED` verdict is a prompt to CHECK, not an order to drop** — the verdict is evidence about what BACKLOG records, and only the owner's word actually closes an owner-desk item. `OPEN-DESK-ONLY` is the normal resting state of a live desk item (17 of 22), not a defect. Update "Robin owes" honestly (close what he delivered, add what emerged). Commit as `s<N> handoff: <summary>`. 🧹 **AND WRITE YOUR ONE-SHOT SPLICE SCRATCH TO `/tmp`, NOT `scripts/` — `scripts/` IS A RUN SURFACE, AND EVERY HELPER A FIRE LEAVES THERE DEGRADES A LANE INSTRUMENT (F-1665-1, measured s1665).** The lock/handoff/ledger/desk helpers a fire writes to splice `STATUS.md` and `goals.json` (the backtick-prose law's prescribed technique) have been committed into `scripts/` by **38 fires, and 151 of the 421 tracked files there — 35.9% — are now single-fire scratch.** `scripts` is one of the nine entries in `lane-usable.mjs`'s `RUN_SURFACE_BASE` (renamed s2349; the surface is now those nine PLUS the gate roots derived from `main:package.json` — F-2349-1) (cite the CODE, the coordinate drifts), whose design comment calls the list *"deliberately narrow: only surfaces that can change whether a task runs or how it is gated"* and excludes `docs/ tasks/ artifacts/ reviews/ logs/` **BY DESIGN**. 📊 **Measured on the live fleet: lane/b's run-surface drift reads 5 files, ALL FIVE s1664 lock/handoff scratch — a 100% false signal** — which suppresses the ✅ *"NONE of it is run-surface … nothing here can stop a task running"* green at `lane-usable.mjs:559` (**was `:540` s2366–s2489 — re-based s2490 by RE-GREPPING the cited content (`if (drift.length === 0) {`, 540 → 559, **+19**), the rot caused BY THE F-2490-1 CURE LANDED IN THIS VERY FILE: `dirt()`'s `--untracked-files=all` flag and its reasoning block sit ~270 lines above it. The FIFTH CONSECUTIVE rot of this coordinate caused by a fire editing the very file the law cites, predicted before committing and cured in its own landing fire. ⓘ **The shift sizes are now +53, +10, +79, +19 — a spread of 69 with no trend, so no remembered delta could ever have been right; and note that a fire whose whole subject was *"a summary view is a lossy projection"* still had to re-measure its own pointer by hand, because a coordinate is a summary view too.** Re-grepped, then eye-checked by reading line 559 back: it printed the `if (drift.length === 0) {` line itself, which is the only proof the new number lands on the right line; was `:461` s2349–s2365 — re-based s2366 by RE-GREPPING the cited content (`if (drift.length === 0) {`, 461 → 540, **+79, THE LARGEST SHIFT THIS COORDINATE HAS EVER TAKEN**), the rot caused BY THE F-2366-1 CURE LANDED IN THIS VERY FILE: the stale-holder classifier and its reasoning block sit ~80 lines above it. Predicted before committing and cured in the same fire. ⓘ **This is the fourth consecutive rot of this coordinate caused by a fire editing the very file the law cites, and the shift sizes so far are +53, +10, +79 — a spread that makes the point better than any warning: there is NO safe constant, and a fire that carried ANY remembered delta forward would have been wrong here by tens of lines. Re-grep, then eye-check with `sed -n <N>p`; the guard proves a pointer MOVED, never that your new number lands on the right line.**; was `:451` FOR ONE HOUR IN s2349, and `:398` s1665–s2348 — re-based TWICE IN ONE FIRE by RE-GREPPING the cited content (`if (drift.length === 0) {`, 398 → 451 → 461), both rots caused BY THIS FIRE'S OWN EDITS to the very file the law cites: first the F-2349-1 derived-run-surface block (+53), then its stderr-leak fix (+10). ⓘ **This is the two-edits-in-one-fire case the GZ-01 clause warns about in its own coordinate, and it bit here exactly as predicted: a fire that re-based once and then carried `+53` forward in its head would have written `:504` and handed the next reader a coordinate 43 lines past the citation. Predicted before committing and cured in the same fire; cite the CODE, the coordinate drifts**) and prints an amber telling the next fire to go prove ancestry for dead files; lanes a/c/d read 18/74/34 where only 13/60/20 are real. ⚠️ **DO NOT CURE THIS BY SWEEPING `scripts/tmp-*` — THE OBVIOUS CURE INVERTS ITS OWN METRIC, AND IT IS FORBIDDEN TWICE OVER.** (1) **It makes the symptom dramatically worse:** `git diff --name-only <branch> main -- scripts` lists paths differing in **PRESENCE** as well as content, so deleting a file makes it drift from the other side — measured, a 125-file sweep takes lane/b from **5 → 126** and lanes a/c/d to **139 / 186 / 146**, until all four lanes are refreshed. (2) **26 of the 151 are CITED RETAINED EVIDENCE, not debris:** `tmp-s1046-unshipped.mjs` alone is cited **24×**, is named in a ratified master's NO list as *"a historical artefact retired by comment under the RETENTION LAW; read it, cite it, **never edit or delete it**"*, and is the subject of a live pre-flight `grep -Fc` that a sweep would turn into a false STOP; `gate-battery.mjs:16` cites `tmp-s1146-gate.mjs:51` **by line**. (3) The RETENTION LAW and `CLAUDE.md` §4.10 both say supersede/move, never erase. ➡️ **THEREFORE THE CURE IS AT THE GENERATOR AND COSTS NOTHING: write splice helpers to `/tmp/s<N>-*.mjs`,** outside the repo, where they can never enter the run surface (s1665 wrote every one of its own there; s1045's handoff already called its untracked lock scratch *"harmless"*). ⓘ **A probe that PRODUCED EVIDENCE is the opposite case and still belongs in `scripts/`, committed and cited** — that is the distinction the corpus already draws, and those 26 files are exactly it. 🚫 **No guard is proposed and none should be built:** "one-shot helper vs retained probe" is a judgement, so a red guard would fire on every legitimate probe and be excused into uselessness within a week (the `cross-engine` label's fate, F-1460-1). 🧯 **AND IF YOU DRAINED A MASTER THIS FIRE, SWEEP YOUR OWN OUTGOING LINE-1 FOR ITS NAME BEFORE YOU COMMIT — A DISPATCH INSTRUCTION IS PERISHABLE ACROSS THE VERY DRAIN THAT DISCHARGES IT, AND THE DRAINING FIRE IS THE ONE MOST LIKELY TO CARRY IT FORWARD STALE (F-2373-1, measured s2373).** 🔑 **THE MECHANISM IS THE HANDOFF'S OWN ECONOMY: a fire copies its closing sections forward rather than re-deriving them, so a drain performed EARLIER IN THE SAME FIRE silently invalidates a sentence living in a section the fire never re-reads.** 📊 **MEASURED: s2371 drained `b4b65f0efaa135eb1be34a31661028206f702444` at 17:15, flipping `lane-c-emdash-player-copy-and-guard-denominator.md`'s leaf to `status="merged"` — and its OWN handoff still read *"the very master (E)(A) tells you to dispatch"* and called it *"my banked em-dash master"*. s2372 then copied that section forward again AND re-attributed s2369's `6dbfb83d3` to itself.** ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: realised cost ZERO and this is NOT a false green — s2373 ran §2E's prescribed `drain-block-check --queue` and got `⛔ ALREADY SHIPPED — DO NOT QUEUE` in one second, exactly as the law intends. The defence held.** 🎯 **What earns it a clause is the DIRECTION and the SURVIVAL: the stale instruction points at Mistake #8, the 824k Flail — this catalog's single most expensive recorded failure — and it survived TWO consecutive fires, one of which was the fire that merged it.** ➡️ **THE DUTY, and it is one command: before committing the handoff, `grep -F "<basename of every master you drained>"` your composed line-1, and retire any instruction naming it as work still to do — in the SAME commit that records the drain, per §4's own end-state rule.** 🔎 **THE TELL, worth more than the duty because it generalises to every carried section: A COPIED SECTION CARRIES ITS PREDECESSOR'S COMMIT HASHES. If you are about to write a hash you did not produce this fire, you are copying rather than re-deriving — and every claim in the sentence around it inherits its predecessor's expiry date, not yours.** 🚫 **NO GUARD, and the restraint is measured rather than lazy: *which masters does a handoff name for dispatch* is prose plus judgement, so a red would fire on every legitimate mention of a master and be excused into uselessness inside a week (F-1460-1, the `cross-engine` fate). ⓘ `banked-master-preflight-guard` is CORRECT and was green 6/6 throughout — its denominator is `planned`/`queued` leaves, so a merged leaf drops out on its own; it never claimed the master was dispatchable and it is not the defect.** 💡 **AND IT IS THIS FACTORY'S MOST-REPEATED LESSON ON A NEW AXIS (F-2153-1 · F-2204-1 · F-2350-1 · F-2360-1 · F-2365-1): those five are cures with no reader. This is the inverse — a READER with no expiry date. An instruction that was TRUE when written needs no cure, only a re-measurement at the moment it is passed on, and Mistake #4 governs your OWN prose exactly as it governs an inherited claim.**

(() => true)` form the master prescribed.** ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: realised cost ZERO, no fire ever ran it, this is NOT a false green, and the layered defences all held — the filename SELF-LABELS `PARKED`, the runner never reads `queue-paused` (0 references in `lane-runner-v3.sh`), and §2E already prescribes the `--queue` check that refuses in one second.** 🎯 **WHAT EARNS IT A CURE IS THE DIRECTION PLUS A LIVE, RECURRING TRIGGER: the destination is `tasks/queue/main/`, the runner dispatches whatever lands there with NO shipped-check of its own (its single `drain-block-check` mention is a COMMENT at `lane-runner-v3.sh:391` — verified by reading, not grepped), and §2E's independent trigger *"re-queue paused main items (queue-paused/) once the pile is below 2"* fires on EVERY dry board, today's included (pile 0). That is Mistake #8 — the 824k Flail, this catalog's most expensive recorded failure — one `mv` away, with a stale instruction pointing at it.** ➡️ **THE STANDING RULE THAT REPLACES IT: `queue-paused/` is NOT a resume queue, it is a PARKING LOT whose occupants change. Before moving anything out of it, run `node scripts/drain-block-check.mjs <master> --queue` and obey the verdict — the directory being non-empty is not evidence that its contents are owed.** ⓘ **The parked file is deliberately LEFT AS s2382 NAMED IT: it is untracked scratch, and renaming it would erase another fire's own labelling of its own finding (supersede, never erase).** 💡 **REUSABLE: a standing order that names its subjects in PROSE but selects them with a GLOB is two different instructions, and only the prose expires legibly. When you retire the last item a wildcard was written for, retire the wildcard in the same breath — otherwise it silently re-aims at whatever next occupies the directory, and the newcomer inherits an authority nobody granted it.**
- Robin owes (nag politely in handoffs, never block on it): lane-d attempt-3 verdict (3a recommended), turret-feel + water-feel playtests, Mac full-regression evidence, favicon 16px eyeball.
- Canon guardrails always: brief §9 (no firearms — frontier-tech only; illustrated never gory; no Native American enemies; naming per §9.4; the agent is "the Prospector").

Measured: **four player-visible slices merged attended-side on the morning of 2026-08-08 (`99f0d60a40…` f-door-1, `38615d7841…` f-door-2, `5698e6ae30…` f-bal-1, `3dec093294…` mp-07b) — all four with review files, all four with ZERO news items**, while all twelve most-recent touches of the queue file were fires at drain time. Nothing in the pipeline was broken; the trigger just never fired. ➡️ **THEREFORE, AS A STANDING SWEEP: any fire backfills any real-change merge whose review exists and whose hash is absent from `gazette-queue.md`** (`grep` the hash — that is the whole check, and it costs one command). ⚠️ **BUT GREP THE 8-CHAR SHORT HASH ACROSS THE WHOLE OF `marketing/outbox/`, NEVER THE FULL HASH AGAINST `gazette-queue.md` ALONE — THE PIPELINE HAS TWO OWNER-FACING SINKS AND THE PRESCRIBED CHECK IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO ONE OF THEM (F-1600-1, measured s1600).** The daily TK-01 ticker digest below reaches the owner exactly as a GAZETTE item does, so a merge headlined there is REPORTED, not owed. 📐 **The two sinks cite hashes in DISJOINT conventions, which is what makes the blindness categorical rather than a sampling artifact — measured across the live outbox: ticker digests carry 618 short hashes and ZERO full-40 ones, while `gazette-queue.md` carries 86 full and 105 short.** A full-40 grep therefore *cannot* match a ticker digest in principle, no matter how many fires run it. 📊 **Cost of the narrow check, window 2026-08-05→s1600: 81 player-path-touching first-parent merges, of which the narrow check calls 47 ABSENT — 20 of those are headlined in a ticker digest and 1 sits in the gazette under its short hash, so 21 of 47 are FALSE POSITIVES (44.7%) and only 26 are genuinely unreported.** ⓘ **The damage is not wasted greps, it is an item that can never be discharged:** `3b2b33c29` ("the county's first standing — V4 Pro secures the-claim via the public door") was left as judgement cost again.** 💡 **THIS REPRICES ANY ABSENCE COUNT TAKEN THE NARROW WAY, INCLUDING THE ONE IN THE F-1599-1 CLAUSE ABOVE** — if ~45% of such a set is already published, the backlog it implies A fire that re-bases by ADDING the remembered delta rather than by RE-GREPPING the content will be off by one and hand the next fire a coordinate that points at a bullet of my item instead of the citation.** Re-based s2281 TWICE IN ONE FIRE by RE-GREPPING the cited content (`1430 → 1436 → 1442`, +6 each) when that fire filed GZ-01 items for the assayer-environment-honesty and door-tick-ceiling-all-epochs merges — the TENTH AND ELEVENTH CONSECUTIVE rots of this coordinate caused by a fire obeying the very duty this clause states, both predicted and cured in their own landing fire. ⓘ **s2281 is the THIRD fire to hit the two-item case (after s2270 and s2279), and it confirms the warning is load-bearing rather than theoretical: its two items were filed ~25 minutes apart around two separate drains, which is exactly the spacing that makes a remembered delta tempting.** Each coordinate was ALSO verified by eye against `sed -n '<N>p'` before being written down, because the guard only proves a pointer MOVED — it cannot prove the new number lands on the right line. ⓘ **TEN CONSECUTIVE +6 SHIFTS NOW, WHICH IS THE PATTERN'S OWN WARNING: the CONSTANCY IS THE TRAP.** A five-line item plus its blank separator has been the house shape for ten fires running, so the remembered delta is RIGHT often enough to feel safe and is wrong exactly when someone writes a four- or six-line item — s2238's +7 is the recorded counter-example. **Re-grep anyway; the cost is one command and the failure is a coordinate that points into the middle of your own item.** Re-based s2280 by RE-GREPPING the cited content (`1424 → 1430`, +6) when that fire filed its GZ-01 item for the door-tick-ceiling-v2 merge — the NINTH CONSECUTIVE rot of this coordinate caused by a fire obeying the very duty this clause states, and the ninth to be predicted and cured in its own landing fire. cite the CONTENT, the coordinate drifts**) as `e7bb88cf`, and the other nine in backticked hash runs in the 08-05/08-06 ticker digests. ⚠️ **This costs MORE than the blindness it resembles: a false-absent set gets BACKFILLED, so the owner receives duplicate news for merges already reported — worse than doing nothing.** ➡️ **THEREFORE THE REAL CURE IS NOT A BETTER NEEDLE — IT IS TO STOP HAND-ROLLING THE GREP: RUN `node scripts/gazette-backfill-sweep.mjs`.** It has existed since s1608 (F-1608-1), it indexes citations **verbatim and tests by PREFIX** — so it is immune to this defect by construction, at any abbreviation width on either side — and since s1613 it prints three numbers (`reported / dismissed / candidates`) with the dismissed list, WARN-level, exit 0 in every state per this clause's own prohibition. **The prose above describes a manual check the factory no longer needs; a fire that reads it as instructions rather than as rationale is the one that gets the wrong answer.** Any fixed width is a bet on someone else's convention, and the outbox is genuinely mixed (86 full + 105 short in the gazette, 618 short in the digests). ✅ **SWEEP STATE: DISCHARGED at s1633 — 0 candidates, confirmed TWICE by independent methods: my hand 8-char truncation over 63 `src`/`assets` merges → 0, and the instrument over its own wider 67-merge player-path set → `reported 63 / dismissed 4 / candidates 0`.** The duty was carried forward as "still owed" by s1631 and s1632 after s1600/s1608/s1610 had already run it, and every drain since s1607 files its GZ-01 item inline at drain-bookkeeping time (visible in the queue file's own log). **Re-measure before carrying it again — this is a Mistake #4 magnet, and the carry costs a fire's whole budget.** Batch backfills into a single `## ROUNDUP —` item per the ≤3/week rule, and break out separately only a merge that carries a live OWNER CHOICE, which is the one thing a roundup can bury. ⚠️ **DO NOT mechanise this as a red guard that reds on any absent hash** — "player-visible" is a judgement, so such a guard fires on every bookkeeping and infrastructure merge and gets excused into uselessness within a week (the `cross-engine` label's fate, F-1460-1). A WARN-level list is the most that should ever be built. — {ledger-voice headline ≤80 chars, ≤3 plain lines, merge hash, screenshot/artifact ref}. Frontier speak per the 063 voice law; NEVER token/price talk; bugs-on-camera get an owner-choice note. ≤3 GAZETTE items/week reach the owner (batch the rest into the weekly roundup item). 🔢 **AND THAT BUDGET IS A *WEEKLY AGGREGATE* THAT NO SINGLE FIRE CAN SEE — SO ASK THE SWEEP, WHICH NOW COUNTS IT FOR YOU, BEFORE YOU FILE (F-2491-1, measured and cured s2491).** A fire files ONE item, for ITS OWN drain, and that act is locally correct **every single time**. The rule's subject is the WEEK, and until this fire nothing in the loop observed it: `gazette-backfill-sweep` counted `reported / dismissed / candidates` and had **no opinion whatever** on how many headlines the week already held. 📊 **MEASURED s2491 for ISO week 2026-W36: `18` standalone items against a budget of `3` — six times over — filed by ELEVEN SEPARATE FIRES (s2459 · s2460 · s2461 · s2462 · s2468 · s2469 · s2470 · s2471 · s2472 · s2473 · s2476), each judging its own merge worth a headline.** ⓘ **Fires DO batch when they think of it — `6` ROUNDUP-CLASS items sit in the very same window, and their own prose says *"the week's three headline slots are spent"* — so this is not neglect. THE COUNTING WAS A HABIT, NOT A STEP**, and during a high-drain burst every fire independently reached the same locally-correct answer. ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: NOT a false green, nothing is broken, and NO VERDICT ANYWHERE WAS WRONG — every one of those 18 items is honest news about a real merge. The queue is a QUEUE the owner approves FROM, so an over-full queue publishes nothing to the family.** 🎯 **What earns it a clause is the DIRECTION: it fails toward MORE owner load, in the one pipeline whose entire purpose is to BOUND owner load — the owner's "one morning action" is handed 18 headlines to weigh where the rule promises 3.** ➡️ **THE DUTY IS NOW ONE LINE OF THE SWEEP YOU ALREADY RUN: it prints `weekly headline budget (<ISO week>): N standalone, M batched` on EVERY run including the happy path (F-2208-1), and when the slots are spent it names what is already standing so the warning is actionable rather than a scold. If N > 3, file as `ROUNDUP-CLASS` — your one item genuinely does look fine alone, which is exactly why the aggregate is the thing to read.** 🚫 **ADVISORY, exit 0 always, and the restraint is INHERITED rather than re-argued: this tool's own header says "player-visible" is a JUDGEMENT, so a red fires on honest bookkeeping and is excused into uselessness inside a week (F-1460-1, the `cross-engine` fate). The COUNT, however, is mechanical — which is precisely why it belongs in the instrument rather than in a sentence, and is the one thing separating this cure from the twenty that correctly declined a guard.** 🔒 **Guarded by `scripts/gazette-weekly-budget-guard.test.mjs` — 13 arms, 1.3 s, rooted in `test:ledger-guards`; `gate-caller-audit` PASS.** 🔬 **TEETH, 11 variants manufactured on scratch copies with the subject restored byte-identical, and per s2226 the sweep doubled as a reachability audit: ALL 13 ARMS red under at least one variant, so none is decoration — but only after TWO more variants were built for two arms that the first pass reddened with nothing.** ⚠️ **THREE METHOD NOTES PAID FOR IN THIS FIRE, and the first is the one worth carrying. (1) THE SWEEP REFUTED AN ARM I HAD JUST WRITTEN AND NAMED AFTER A REAL FINDING: I added an arm claiming to defend F-2391-1 (the UTC/local date-string trap), manufactured that exact defect — `Date.UTC` swapped for a parsed date STRING — and it **reddened NOTHING**. The subject takes y/m/d INTEGERS, so the hazard is unreachable BY CONSTRUCTION; the safety is a property of the SIGNATURE, not of any assertion. The arm was renamed to what it actually tests, because **an arm named for a defence it cannot provide is worse than no arm — it makes the next reader stop looking.** (2) MY FIRST CENSUS WAS A FLOOR, NOT A COUNT: dating items from the sweep's own `rows` (player-path merges inside `--since`) silently drops every item citing a CONTAINED commit, which the existing roundups genuinely do — a week cited entirely that way would report `0 standalone` and NOT warn, a false clean rather than a conservative under-count. Dating from the whole history is `12,563` commits in **~216 ms**, so the honest read is also the cheap one; an arm now reds on that exact regression. (3) MY OWN TEETH HARNESS UNDER-REPORTED COVERAGE: its `^✖ (.+?) \(` matcher truncated every arm name at the first ` (`, so arms whose names carry a parenthetical scored ZERO and looked like decoration. **A reachability audit is itself an instrument, and its blind spot is indistinguishable from the absence of coverage it is built to find.**
An engine-era registry bump is player-visible news by definition (`tasks/engine-era-law-v3.md`).
DAILY (first fire after 06:00 local): compile yesterday's merges into ONE ticker digest appended to marketing/outbox/ticker-digest-<date>.md (micro-headlines ≤140 chars + hashes). The owner approves digests in one morning action. Publication stays owner-only, always. 🕰️ **AND VALIDATE YOUR DATE WINDOW ON A DAY YOU KNOW IS BUSY BEFORE YOU BELIEVE ANY ZERO IT REPORTS — A UTC/LOCAL SLIP COLLAPSES THE WINDOW TO ZERO WIDTH AND RETURNS AN AFFIRMATIVE `0` FOR EVERY DAY ALIKE (F-2391-1, measured s2391).** This machine is **UTC+07**, so a window built the obvious way — `new Date(Date.parse(d + 'T00:00:00+07:00') + 86400000).toISOString().slice(0,10)` — renders `+07` midnight as **17:00Z the PREVIOUS day**, and the derived end equals the start. 📊 **MEASURED: that probe returned `0 first-parent commits` for 2026-08-27, 08-28 AND 08-29 — and the 08-29 digest, sitting on disk, records `95`.** The correct form (pure integer calendar arithmetic on the y/m/d components, NO `Date` parsing of the window bounds) reproduces `92 · 1 · 0 · 95 · 121` across 08-26→08-30 and **exactly re-derives the recorded judgement** that 08-27 holds one commit (a fire's lock line) and 08-28 none. ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: realised cost ZERO, and NO SHIPPED TOOL HAS THIS BUG** — `ledger-mirror-freshness.mjs` avoids `Date` parsing on purpose and says so, which is where this trap was already named for its own two scripts. This was an **ad-hoc triage probe**, the kind a fire types by hand and no battery observes (the F-1582-1 gap). 🎯 **What earns it a clause is the DIRECTION and the UNIFORMITY: the answer is not off by a day, it is `0` for EVERY subject — the empty-corpus false green this file has spent twenty fires curing, arriving through a TIMEZONE rather than a `catch`, a guard, a cwd or a filename. A quiet-day verdict and a 95-commit day are byte-identical, so the probe is right exactly when you are lucky.** ➡️ **THE CURE IS ONE ARM AND IT IS F-2215-1's RULE APPLIED TO A WINDOW RATHER THAN TO A TOOL: before trusting a zero, run the same probe over a day whose count you already know is non-zero. If THAT comes back `0` too, the window is broken, not the day.** 🚫 **No guard is proposed and the restraint is measured: there is no shipped artifact to guard, and "did a fire hand-type a correct date window?" is unobservable to every battery — the same reason F-1669-1 declined a mechanism. This is a READING habit, like §2.0c's runner check.** 💡 **REUSABLE: a corpus can go empty from a TIMEZONE as easily as from a name, a cwd or a stale tree — and a window is a selector nobody in this streak has been keyed to check. Whenever a probe reports "nothing happened", ask what it would have reported had something happened.** ⏫ **AMENDED s2562 (F-2562-2) — THAT CURE IS NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT, AND I PROVED IT BY PASSING IT WHILE MEASURING THE WRONG DAY. A BUSY-DAY CONTROL ESTABLISHES THAT A WINDOW IS *LIVE*, NEVER THAT IT IS *ALIGNED* — AND A SHIFTED WINDOW IS BUSY TOO.** 🔑 **THE MECHANISM IS IN GIT, NOT IN YOUR ARITHMETIC, WHICH IS WHY THIS CLAUSE'S CURE CANNOT REACH IT: git's approxidate fills a BARE `YYYY-MM-DD` with the CURRENT TIME-OF-DAY, so `--since=2026-09-11 --until=2026-09-12` means `[09-11@now, 09-12@now)` and the window SLIDES THROUGH THE DAY — the same command returns different days' commits depending on what o'clock a fire runs it.** This clause prescribes building bounds by integer y/m/d arithmetic, and s2562 DID that and was still wrong, because the defect is in what you HAND TO GIT, not in how you computed it. 📊 **MEASURED BY PREDICTION, 5/5 SUBJECTS, at 16:03 local: `--since=D --until=D+1` returned exactly the commits in `[D@16:03, D+1@16:03)` — 08-27→`0`, 08-28→`68`, 09-11→`8`, 09-12→`2`, 09-13→`29`. GROUND TRUTH, built WITHOUT any `--since`/`--until` by bucketing every first-parent commit on its own `%cI`: 08-27→`1`, 08-28→`0`, 09-11→`0`, 09-12→`8`, 09-13→`2`. Every bare reading is a DIFFERENT DAY's count.** ✅ **AND THE OFFSET-EXPLICIT FORM (`--since=D T00:00:00+07:00`) REPRODUCES THIS CLAUSE'S OWN RECORDED JUDGEMENT TO THE DIGIT — 08-27 holds one commit (a fire's lock line) and 08-28 none — which is the corroboration that makes the correction believable rather than a second guess.** ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: realised cost ZERO, NOT a false green, and NO SHIPPED TOOL HAS THIS BUG — verified by READING all four live users, not inferred: `gazette-backfill-sweep.mjs`'s `normalizeSince` pins `T00:00:00` (its reason was UNDOCUMENTED until s2562 and is now a comment at the site), `red-inventory-lookup.mjs:64` passes a full ISO `startTime`, and `runner-processes.sh:31` / `start-lane-runner.sh:108` pass `@<epoch>`. All immune.** 🎯 **WHAT EARNS IT AN AMENDMENT IS THE DIRECTION, AND IT IS THE OPPOSITE OF F-2391-1'S: that defect returned `0` FOR EVERY DAY and failed toward doing NOTHING. This one returns a PLAUSIBLE NON-ZERO COUNT FOR THE WRONG DAY and fails toward FABRICATING WORK.** s2562 ran a TK-01 series check, read `08-28 → 68 commits, no digest` and `09-11 → 8 commits, no digest`, and was one step from compiling and committing an owner-facing ticker digest for a day that had **zero** commits — and from publishing "TK-01 has two holes" as a finding. **Both days are genuinely empty. The only thing that stopped it was noticing that the 8 commits were the very ones the 09-12 digest already reported — recognition, not method.** ➡️ **THEREFORE THE CONTROL IS UPGRADED, AND IT IS THE ONLY FORM THAT CAN CATCH THIS: build a GROUND TRUTH THAT USES NO WINDOW AT ALL — bucket the commits by their own `%cs`/`%cI` — and assert your windowed count EQUALS it on a known day. A control that shares the window's own mechanism cannot audit the window.** ⓘ **TK-01 itself is DISCHARGED and whole, measured with the corrected form: 61 digests spanning 2026-07-10 → 09-13, and the single coverage day with no digest (08-27) holds one fire LOCK commit, which is correctly not news.** 🚫 **NO GUARD, and the restraint is INHERITED rather than re-argued: this clause already declined one because "did a fire hand-type a correct date window?" is unobservable to every battery (the F-1669-1 reason), and that holds exactly as well for the sliding form. The cure is the sentence you are reading plus the comment now standing at `normalizeSince`.** 💡 **REUSABLE, AND IT IS A NEW AXIS FOR THIS STREAK: twenty-odd findings have asked how a SUBJECT SET can be wrong — too narrow, wrong tree, wrong axis, a registry not a census, entries not files. This one has the right subject set and a control that PASSES WHILE AIMED ELSEWHERE. F-2215-1 taught us to assert that a control PRODUCED something; the rung below it is to assert that it produced something ABOUT THE RIGHT SUBJECT — and the only proof of that is a measurement taken by a method that could not have produced the same error.**

## RT-01 DUTY — the weekly rotation mint (added 2026-09-03, attended — the transfer board's fresh seeds, spec `specs/transfer-board.md` L2/L5)
ONCE PER ISO WEEK, on the first fire after Sunday 00:00 UTC (check whether `assets/rotations/rotation-seeds.json` already holds the rotation whose `opensAt` is the coming Monday; if yes, DISCHARGE with one line): mint it with `node scripts/rotation-mint.mjs --week <YYYY-Www of the coming Monday> --salt-file ~/.goldrush/rotation-salt --append assets/rotations/rotation-seeds.json`, refresh the fenced block in `public/skill.md` between `skillmd-guard:rotations:start/end` with that rotation's JSON, re-pin the skill.md guard baseline per its own instructions, move `CURRENT_ROTATION_ID` in `site/assay-office.js` to the rotation that is OPEN (not the one just minted), commit path-scoped with prefix `rt:`, push, and note it in the handoff. The salt is owner-held and never committed; a missing salt file is a STOP with one desk line, never a default. ⛔ **BUT DO NOT MINT ON THE SUNDAY — THIS DUTY'S OWN TRIGGER AND THE GATE THAT GUARDS ITS OUTPUT ARE JOINTLY UNSATISFIABLE, AND THE ONLY THING THAT HAS EVER PROTECTED THE FACTORY IS THAT THE ONE REAL MINT RAN LATE (F-2568-1, measured s2568).** The trigger above says *"the first fire after Sunday 00:00 UTC"* and the minted rotation's `opensAt` is **the coming Monday**, so at an on-time mint the new rotation **has not opened yet** — while `scripts/skillmd-guard.test.mjs:103` (cite the CODE, the coordinate drifts) asserts `` `const CURRENT_ROTATION_ID = '${rotations.rotations.at(-1).id}';` `` — i.e. the **newest MINTED**, which `rotation-mint.mjs:53` sorts by `opensAt` so `at(-1)` is always the one that opens LAST. **The law says OPEN; the gate says NEWEST; on the Sunday those are different rotations.** 📊 **PROVEN BY MANUFACTURING, the predicate DERIVED from the guard's own source line rather than transcribed, with the live tree asserted as a passing CONTROL first (F-2215-1): at an on-time mint of `r2026w39` on Sunday 2026-09-20T07:00Z — obey RT-01 (`CURRENT='r2026w38'`, the open one) → **guard RED**; obey the guard (`CURRENT='r2026w39'`) → **guard GREEN and the public board points at a rotation that has not opened**. REVERSE CONTROL, a LATE mint after `opensAt`: the two **AGREE**, no contradiction.** ⚙️ **AND THAT REVERSE CONTROL IS THE WHOLE EXPLANATION OF WHY THIS HAS NEVER FIRED: the only real mint, `66b782b09`, landed 2026-09-14T04:21Z against an `opensAt` of 00:00Z — `4h22m` LATE — so `at(-1)` WAS the open one. `r2026w37` was the bootstrap with no predecessor to point at. Both escapes are accidents.** ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: realised cost ZERO, nothing is broken, no verdict anywhere was wrong, and this is NOT a false green — a guard red is LOUD.** 🎯 **What earns it a clause is the DIRECTION and a DATED trigger: `skillmd-guard.test.mjs` is a leg of `test:node-guards`, a mandated gate, so a fire obeying this duty ON TIME gets a red and the obvious way to "fix" the red is to point the family's browser at a rotation that has not opened. The next mint is `r2026w39`, trigger Sunday `2026-09-20`, and fires run every five minutes — so an on-time mint is the DEFAULT outcome, not an unlucky one.** ➡️ **THEREFORE, BINDING UNTIL THE OWNER RULES: mint ON OR AFTER the Monday `opensAt` (which is what the working practice already does), never on the Sunday — that is the only window in which the law and the gate are jointly satisfiable. And NEVER resolve a red `skillmd-guard` by moving `CURRENT_ROTATION_ID` onto a rotation whose `opensAt` is in the future.** ⓘ **The tradeoff is named rather than hidden: between Monday 00:00Z and the mint, `CURRENT_ROTATION_ID` still names the rotation that just CLOSED, and `functions/api/standings.ts:492–493` accepts any listed rotation with **no window check at all**. That gap is bounded by the fire cadence (~5 min) where the Sunday mint's is a guaranteed 24 h, which is why this is the safer of the two — not because it is clean.** 🔺 **THE REAL FIX IS AN OWNER/ATTENDED CALL AND IS ON THE DESK AS F-2568-2: abolish the hand-moved constant and compute the open rotation from the seeds, exactly as `functions/api/standings.ts:1188` (`currentOrLatestRotation`) ALREADY DOES — filter `opensAt <= now`, take the latest. The correct answer is already derivable from data this repo ships; only the landing page re-states it by hand, once a week, forever.** 🚫 **NO GUARD CHANGE, and the restraint is measured rather than lazy: making the pin time-aware would red during every lawful Sunday→Monday window and be excused into uselessness inside a week (F-1460-1, the `cross-engine` fate), and abolishing the constant is a player-facing `site/` change while the CODEX-WALL suspends §2E authoring and routes implementation to the attended session. The cure is the sentence you are reading, at the site.** 💡 **REUSABLE, and it is a NEW AXIS for this streak: twenty-odd findings have asked how a MEASUREMENT can be wrong — a swallow, a guard, a cwd, a filename, a timezone, a registry, a clock. THIS ONE IS A *DUTY* AND A *GATE* THAT ARE EACH INDIVIDUALLY CORRECT AND CANNOT BOTH BE OBEYED. Neither is a defect on its own; the defect is the pair, and no instrument in this factory examines pairs. When a duty writes a value that a gate pins, ask whether the duty's own SCHEDULE can put those two out of agreement — and if the only evidence they agree is that the duty has always run late, you are being protected by an accident.**

## LB-01 DUTY — the county book's daily offsite pull (added 2026-08-24, attended — l4 follow-up #2)
ONCE PER COVERAGE DAY (the TK-01 cadence — check whether `artifacts/ledger-backups/` already holds today's `ledger-YYYY-MM-DD.db`; if yes, DISCHARGED, move on): run `node scripts/ledger-backup-pull.mjs`. It is idempotent and bounded; box unreachable = WARN in the handoff, never a red, retry next fire. If it pulled a new file: `git add artifacts/ledger-backups/<file>` path-scoped + commit (`ops: LB-01 ledger mirror <date>`) + push — origin IS the offsite copy (the l4 design: no new secrets on the box). ⚠️ STANDING GATE from the l4 runner's own note: BEFORE the accounts flip onto sqlite, this raw mirror must become an ENCRYPTED artifact — a plaintext ledger of county standings is fine, account data is not. That flip's master must name this line. 🔒 **AND THAT GATE NOW HAS AN INSTRUMENT INSTEAD OF ONLY A SENTENCE — RUN `node scripts/ledger-mirror-exposure.mjs` (F-2353-1, measured and cured s2353).** The gate sat here as PROSE from 2026-08-24 with **zero enforcement surface**, and the manual key-read s2352 added below covers the `--fill-gaps` arm ONLY — so the DAILY arm, the one whose output this duty tells you to `git add` + commit + **push**, had neither an instruction nor an instrument. ⚖️ **NOTHING IS EXPOSED TODAY, measured not assumed: all 6 mirrors hold `kv` only, 32 keys, ZERO account-class rows.** 🚪 **What earns it a tool is that the door is ONE-WAY: a committed account row can only be undone by a history rewrite, i.e. a force-push, which is deny-listed here.** 🔑 **AND THE GATE IS HELD SHUT BY AN OUTAGE, NOT BY DESIGN — F-2353-2 measured the routing rather than inferring it: `wrangler.toml` binds NO `ACCOUNTS`, `ops/droplet/agenttown.app.nginx.conf:17–24` proxies `/api/(request-code|verify|session|save/|delete-account)` to the local sqlite ledger at `127.0.0.1:8791`, and `ops/droplet/ledger-backup.mjs:6` mirrors that SAME `/opt/goldrush-ledger/ledger.db` wholesale.
