That question is answerable only about TODAY: **once today's file lands, every future fire reads DISCHARGED and a gap behind it never becomes today again.** No amount of diligence closes this, because the question is never asked. 📊 **MEASURED s2351, and the gap was live: `ledger-2026-08-27.db` is ABSENT from the series (24 · 25 · 26 · __ · 28 · 29).** The 08-27 fire log carries **22 FIRE STARTs and ZERO mentions** of `LB-01` or `ledger-backup-pull`; so does 08-28's — the duty simply did not run, and the 08-28 mirror was finally committed at **2026-08-29T01:33+07:00, a day late**. ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED — THIS IS NOT A DATA-LOSS FINDING: the realised cost is ZERO.** The mirror is a CUMULATIVE sqlite DB; 08-26 and 08-28 are genuinely distinct backups (**different sha256, verified — not merely the same 618496-byte size**) that bracket the hole, and player sign-in was DOWN 2026-08-24→08-29 (F-MAIL-0829), so no new standings could have been written on the missing day. **Nothing was lost.** 🎯 **WHAT EARNS IT A CLAUSE IS THE DIRECTION AND THE FACT THAT NOBODY COULD HAVE KNOWN EITHER WAY: an offsite backup is the one artifact whose entire purpose is to be there on the day the disk dies (Mistake #11, "The Missing Remote" — weeks of work on one disk, no origin). A backup series that silently stops being refreshed while every prescribed probe reports success is that mistake rebuilt one layer up.** 🔎 **AND NOTHING WATCHED IT — VERIFIED BY READING, NOT INFERRED: `artifacts/ledger-backups/` has ZERO consumers anywhere in `scripts/`, `ops/` or `package.json` outside the pull script itself, `health-watch.sh` does not touch it, and LB-01 appears in exactly ONE law surface line (this one) — not in `CLAUDE.md`, not in any skill.** That is F-2153-1 and F-2204-1 a fourth time: **the duty had exactly one reader and no instrument.** ➡️ **THEREFORE, AS PART OF THE LB-01 DUTY: run `node scripts/ledger-mirror-freshness.mjs`** — it names every MISSING interior day and the AGE of the newest mirror, declares its corpus on stdout ALWAYS including the happy path (F-2208-1), and separates **2 = "could not answer"** from **1 = "answered, and the answer refuses"** under `--strict`. 🚫 **IT IS ADVISORY AND EXITS 0 IN EVERY STATE BY DEFAULT, and that restraint is measured rather than lazy: a gap is frequently LAWFUL** (the box is down, the machine slept, the droplet's own cron had not yet run, a fire cycle went to a drain), **so a red would fire during ordinary correct operation and be excused into uselessness inside a week — the `cross-engine` fate this file names at F-1460-1. It is a triage READ, exactly as §2.0c's runner check is, not a gate.** ⓘ **A hole is a JUDGEMENT, not a backfill order: the droplet keeps only its own retention window and may no longer hold the day. Record the judgement where the next fire reads it.** 🔒 **Guarded by `scripts/ledger-mirror-freshness-guard.test.mjs` — 15 arms, rooted in `test:ledger-guards`.** ⚠️ **EVERY ARM WAS PROVEN BY MANUFACTURING THE DEFECT, and the sweep doubled as a reachability audit per s2226's duty: 11 variants, and after the first pass FOUR arms (1, 3, 11, 14) had been reddened by NOTHING — i.e. were candidate decoration — so four more variants were built until every one of the 15 reds under at least one.** Each over-general cure is caught by exactly the reverse control built for it: **re-anchoring the corpus to `process.cwd()` reds arm 9 ALONE** (the subject anchors to `import.meta.url` ON PURPOSE, so the only way to aim it at a fixture is to RELOCATE it — which is what makes that arm a genuine control), and **deriving "today" from `toISOString()` reds arm 15 ALONE.** 🧭 **THAT LAST ONE IS THE DATE TRAP, AND IT IS WORTH KNOWING BEFORE YOU EDIT EITHER FILE: `new Date('2026-08-27')` parses as UTC MIDNIGHT while `new Date()` is LOCAL, and this machine is UTC+07.** `ledger-backup-pull.mjs` computes its own "today" as `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` — **in UTC** — so between 00:00 and 07:00 local it is already reasoning about YESTERDAY. ⚖️ **That skew is NOT claimed as a defect and was deliberately left alone: the droplet's backup lands ~09:30 local, well inside the window where the two agree, and the script self-corrects later the same day. It is named ONLY so the next reader does not "fix" the freshness probe into agreement with it.** The probe therefore does NO `Date` parsing of the series at all — pure integer calendar arithmetic, with "today" from LOCAL components. 💡 **THE REUSABLE HALF: an EXISTENTIAL check ("does today's artifact exist?") and a SERIES check ("is the series whole?") are different questions, and the first is the one that feels like diligence. Whenever a duty is discharged by the presence of one dated artifact, ask what it would take for that check to report a MISSING EARLIER ONE — and if the answer is "nothing could", the duty has no instrument, only a habit.** 🛠️ **AND WHEN IT NAMES A HOLE, YOU CAN NOW CLOSE ONE — `node scripts/ledger-backup-pull.mjs --fill-gaps` (F-2352-2, measured and cured s2352). THE PROBE ABOVE WAS DETECTION WITH NO RECOVERY PATH FOR ITS ENTIRE FIRST DAY.** The default pull asks the box for `ls -1t <dir>/ledger-*.db | head -n 1` — **the single newest file** — and exits early the moment today's copy is present locally, so it is **structurally incapable of ever fetching a day behind today**: a fire reading `⚠️ MISSING COVERAGE DAY(S)` and reaching for the prescribed tool got a no-op, in every mode. That is the ladder shape this file keeps recording (F-2209-1 exit code → F-2210-1 stdout → F-2211-1 capture → F-2217-1 enumeration): **a cure lands at layer N and the layer beneath it cannot act on what layer N reports.** ⚖️ **AND THE CLAUSE ABOVE TOLD YOU NOT TO BOTHER LOOKING** — its closing sentence read *"the droplet keeps only its own retention window and may no longer hold the day."* **MEASURED s2352: `KEEP_DAYS = 14` in `ops/droplet/ledger-backup.mjs`, and the named hole `ledger-2026-08-27.db` was STILL ON THE BOX, two days into that window.** It was recovered, verified and committed, and the probe now reads `✅ WHOLE AND CURRENT`. **A predecessor's REASON for declining is a CLAIM, not a constraint — re-measure it before you inherit the restraint, and never write a hedge that names a MECHANISM you did not check, because the next reader will trust the mechanism and stop measuring.** 🚦 **`--fill-gaps` IS AN EXPLICIT OPT-IN AND MUST STAY ONE: a hole really is a JUDGEMENT, not a backfill order, and the mirror is a CUMULATIVE sqlite DB so a bracketed hole usually costs nothing. A day pulled from the box is not automatically a day this repo may hold in plaintext.** ⛔ **BUT NOT WITH THE COMMAND THIS CLAUSE PRESCRIBED UNTIL s2360 — `sqlite3 <file> "select key from kv"` IS REFUSED BY YOUR OWN GATE, AND THIS WAS THE ONLY REFUSED PRESCRIPTION IN ANY LAW SURFACE WITH NO RUNNABLE PATH STATED (F-2360-1, measured and cured s2360).** 🔬 **CONTROL-ISOLATED, because s2350 records `cd … && …` as a confound and because "unallowlisted" turned out NOT to mean "refused" (see below): `sqlite3 <file> "select key from kv"` BARE → `This command requires approval`; `sqlite3 -version` BARE, no quotes, no file → **also refused**, so it is the BINARY and not the quoting; POSITIVE CONTROLS in the same batch, all bare, all RAN — `sed -n 461p <file>`, `date "+%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ"`, `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -- artifacts/ reviews/`.** ➡️ **RUN THE INSTRUMENT YOU ALREADY HAVE, WHICH NEEDS NO GATED BINARY AT ALL: `node scripts/ledger-mirror-exposure.mjs` (add `--dir <path>` to point it elsewhere).** It reads every `*.db` in the mirror directory through **node's built-in `node:sqlite`** (`DatabaseSync`, `readOnly: true`) rather than the `sqlite3` CLI, so `Bash(node:*)` covers it; it classifies each key against the six `account:`/`attempts:`/`code:`/`ratelimit:`/`save:`/`session:` prefixes **re-derived from `functions/api/_accounts.ts` by its own guard**, so a renamed key class cannot silently fall outside the denylist; and it refuses with `⛔ ACCOUNT DATA IN A PLAINTEXT MIRROR — DO NOT COMMIT, DO NOT PUSH.` **A gap-fill mirror lands IN that directory, so the default invocation already covers it — there is nothing extra to type.** ⚖️ **SEVERITY STATED HONESTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT INFLATED: NOT a false green, and the realised cost is ZERO. And the gate is held shut today by nothing but the sign-in outage: fixing F-MAIL-0829 arms it.** 💡 **THE REUSABLE HALF, and it is this file's most-repeated finding for the FOURTH time (F-2153-1 · F-2204-1 · F-2350-1): THE CURE ALREADY EXISTED, IN A WORKING TOOL, ALREADY ROOTED IN THE MANDATED BATTERY — and the clause that needed it named a refused binary instead. §2.0d states the node fallback generically, but it names `bash` and `npm` and is thousands of words from here, and `sqlite3` is a binary it never mentions. TK-01's digest series is UNBROKEN across all 48 files from its 2026-07-10 inception through 08-26, and its one real three-day miss (`ticker-digest-2026-08-26.md`, born 08-29, lag 3 where the other 47 are lag 1) SELF-HEALED — a fire that was not there when it happened rebuilt it in full from `git log`.** 🔑 **SO "EXISTENTIAL" WAS THE WRONG KEY, AND THE RIGHT ONE IS SHARPER: AN EXISTENTIAL DISCHARGE TEST IS DANGEROUS EXACTLY IN PROPORTION TO THE PERISHABILITY OF ITS SOURCE.** TK-01 reads **git history** — durable, retroactively queryable, never pruned — so a missed day is always reconstructible and the hole closes itself the next time anyone looks. LB-01 reads a **remote box on a 14-day window** — so a missed day is recoverable only until it ages out, and then no tool can help. **Same shape, opposite consequence.** ➡️ **Measured s2352, the perishable-source set for this factory is exactly `{LB-01}`: the repo contains ONE pull-shaped script, `deploy.sh` pushes rather than pulls, and ART/GZ-01/assayer all read durable local or git-resident corpora.
